## 4.9 Sissy Squat / Reverse Nordic

**Category:** Bodyweight · Isolation (quads) · **Level:** Intermediate → Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps (especially rectus femoris)
- **Secondary:** Core, hip flexors (isometric)
- **Specific muscles:** Rectus femoris and vasti at long length
- **Joint actions:** Knee flexion/extension with the hip held extended

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Optional | Sissy-squat bench (optional) | Optional | No | No | Optional (assistance) | Is bodyweight |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Reverse Nordic: kneeling, body straight from knees to head. Sissy squat: standing on toes, holding a support.
- **Foot position:** Kneeling on a pad, feet anchored or free.
- **Hands / grip:** Crossed or holding a band/support.
- **Starting range:** Upright.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Kneel tall, squeeze glutes.
2. **Initiate.** Lean back as one unit from the knees.
3. **Movement path.** Hips stay extended (no bending at hips).
4. **Breathing.** Breathe steadily.
5. **Contraction.** Quads lengthen and work eccentrically.
6. **End position.** Lean back as far as you can control.
7. **Return.** Return by contracting the quads.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Pain-free range; increase gradually.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Straight line knees–hips–shoulders.
- **Tempo:** 3–4 s lowering.
- **Muscle engagement:** Hip-extended position keeps rectus femoris long — a stimulus squats don't provide.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bending at the hips | Squeeze glutes |
| Going too deep too soon | Use a band or partial range |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Partial range 2 × 6–8 | 3 × 8–12 | 3 × 10–15 weighted | 90 s | Range first, then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Fair | Moderate | Excellent | Build up slowly; knee load is high |

- **Advantages:** Unique lengthened quad stimulus without equipment
- **Disadvantages:** Can irritate sensitive knees

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg extension · Dumbbell: Weighted reverse Nordic · Bodyweight: This is the BW option · Easier: Banded reverse Nordic · Harder: Weighted sissy squat

## 4.10 Spanish Squat

**Category:** Resistance band · Knee-friendly · Rehab-oriented · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps
- **Secondary:** Gluteus maximus
- **Specific muscles:** Vasti; patellar tendon loaded isometrically
- **Joint actions:** Knee extension (isometric or slow dynamic) with a vertical shin

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Optional | No | No | Yes (heavy loop behind the knees, anchored) | Wall sit |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Heavy band anchored to a rack, looped behind both knees; lean back into it.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width, shins stay vertical.
- **Hands / grip:** Hold a light weight or arms forward.
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Step back to tension the band.
2. **Initiate.** Sit back, letting the band hold the knees.
3. **Movement path.** Shins stay vertical, torso upright.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe steadily.
5. **Contraction.** Quads hold tension.
6. **End position.** Hold at roughly 60–90° knee flexion.
7. **Return.** Stand up slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Isometric hold 30–45 s, or slow reps.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knees don't move forward.
- **Tempo:** Isometric holds or 3 s down/3 s up.
- **Muscle engagement:** Used in patellar tendinopathy rehab to load the quads with little pain.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Band too light | Use heavy band so you can lean back |
| Leaning forward | Sit back into the band |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3–5 × 30–45 s hold | 3 × 10 slow reps | Weighted holds | 60–90 s | Longer holds, deeper angle, add load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low–moderate | Low | Excellent | Low | Good (band + anchor) | Very knee-friendly |

- **Advantages:** Quad loading for irritable knees
- **Disadvantages:** Not a primary size builder

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg extension isometric · Dumbbell: Goblet wall sit · Bodyweight: Wall sit · Easier: Shallow hold · Harder: Deeper or weighted hold

|---|---|

## 4.11 Step-Up

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral · Functional · **Level:** Beginner → Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps, gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Gluteus medius, adductors, calves
- **Specific muscles:** Vasti, glute max, glute medius (pelvic control)
- **Joint actions:** Knee and hip extension on the working leg

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | Optional | No | No | Bodyweight step-up |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing facing a box; box height so the working thigh is near parallel.
- **Foot position:** Whole working foot on the box.
- **Hands / grip:** Dumbbells at sides.
- **Starting range:** Working foot on box, knee bent.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Foot fully on the box.
2. **Initiate.** Lean slightly forward and drive through the working leg.
3. **Movement path.** Rise to stand on the box without pushing off the back foot.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale up.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze glute at the top.
6. **End position.** Standing tall on the box.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly back down with control (the eccentric matters).
8. **Recommended ROM.** Box ~knee height; lower for beginners.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knee tracks toes; pelvis level.
- **Tempo:** 1 s up, 2–3 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Minimise push from the trailing leg.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bouncing off the back foot | Lift back toes |
| Box too high | Lower the box |
| Knee caving | Slower, lighter |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 8–10 / leg | 3 × 8–12 / leg | 3–4 × 6–10 / leg heavy | 60–90 s | Higher box, more load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Good | Moderate | Excellent | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Highly functional for stairs and running; easy to scale
- **Disadvantages:** Easy to cheat with the back leg

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg press (single leg) · Dumbbell: Dumbbell step-up · Bodyweight: Bodyweight step-up · Easier: Low box · Harder: High box, barbell, lateral step-up

## 4.12 Dumbbell Squat (dumbbells at sides)

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · Bilateral · **Level:** Beginner → Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps, gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Adductors, grip, upper back, core
- **Specific muscles:** Vastus lateralis, medialis, intermedius, glute max, adductor magnus
- **Joint actions:** Knee and hip extension; ankle dorsiflexion on descent

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes (pair) | No | No | No | Bodyweight squat |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing tall, dumbbells hanging at the sides with straight arms, shoulders down and back.
- **Foot position:** Hip- to shoulder-width, toes out 10–20°; elevating the heels on small plates increases knee travel (quad bias).
- **Hands / grip:** Neutral grip (palms facing in); use straps if grip fails before the legs.
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand tall with the dumbbells at your sides.
2. **Initiate.** Take a breath into the belly and brace, then bend the knees and hips together.
3. **Movement path.** Sit straight down between the feet; the dumbbells travel vertically beside the legs; knees track over the toes.
4. **Breathing.** Hold the breath on the way down; exhale as you pass the hardest point on the way up.
5. **Contraction.** Drive through the whole foot, keeping the chest up.
6. **End position.** Stand fully upright.
7. **Return.** Lower again under control.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Until the thighs are at least parallel, or until the dumbbells touch the floor/plates.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Neutral spine, arms long, knees in line with toes.
- **Tempo:** 2–3 s down, 1 s up.
- **Muscle engagement:** Holding the load low makes it easier to stay upright than a barbell back squat; grip is usually the limiter.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Leaning forward and letting the dumbbells drift in front | Keep them beside the legs; heels elevated if needed |
| Shrugging and pulling with the arms | Arms are hooks; shoulders down |
| Short range because the dumbbells hit the floor | Stand on low plates or switch to goblet squat |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 10–12 | 3–4 × 8–15 | 3 × 12–20 heavy, heel-elevated | 90–120 s | Heavier pair; straps; then heel-elevated or barbell squat |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Excellent | Moderate | Excellent | Very safe (just drop the dumbbells) |

- **Advantages:** Needs only dumbbells; easy to bail; low spinal load
- **Disadvantages:** Grip and dumbbell weight cap the load

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg press, hack squat · Dumbbell: Goblet squat · Bodyweight: Bodyweight squat · Easier: Box squat to a bench · Harder: Heel-elevated dumbbell squat, tempo squat

# 5. Hamstring exercise library

The hamstrings both extend the hip and flex the knee. Hinges (RDL) train hip extension; curls train knee flexion and are the **only** way to load the biceps femoris short head. Seated curls, which keep the hamstrings long, have the strongest growth evidence.

| # | Exercise | Category | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | Barbell Romanian Deadlift (RDL) | Compound · Barbell · Hip hinge | Intermediate |
| 5.2 | Dumbbell Romanian Deadlift | Compound · Dumbbell · Hip hinge | Beginner |
| 5.3 | Stiff-Leg Deadlift (dumbbell or barbell) | Compound · Hip hinge | Intermediate |
| 5.4 | Single-Leg Romanian Deadlift | Unilateral · Dumbbell/Kettlebell · Functional | Intermediate |
| 5.5 | Seated Leg Curl | Isolation · Machine | Beginner → Advanced |
| 5.6 | Lying Leg Curl | Isolation · Machine | Beginner |
| 5.7 | Nordic Hamstring Curl | Bodyweight · Eccentric · Injury-prevention | Intermediate → Advanced |
| 5.8 | Glute-Ham Raise (GHR) | Machine · Bodyweight · Posterior chain | Advanced |
| 5.9 | Conventional Deadlift | Compound · Barbell · Posterior chain | Intermediate → Advanced |

## 5.1 Barbell Romanian Deadlift (RDL)

**Category:** Compound · Barbell · Hip hinge · **Level:** Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings (long heads), gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Adductor magnus, erector spinae, grip
- **Specific muscles:** Biceps femoris long head, semitendinosus, semimembranosus, glute max, adductor magnus
- **Joint actions:** Hip extension with a near-fixed, softly bent knee

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Alternative | Yes | No | No | Single-leg RDL (bodyweight) / good-morning with dowel |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing tall holding the bar at the hips, shoulders back, spine neutral.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width, toes forward.
- **Hands / grip:** Double overhand just outside thighs; straps allowed.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Take the bar from a rack at mid-thigh.
- **Starting range:** Standing, knees soft.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand with the bar at the hips, knees softly bent.
2. **Initiate.** Push the hips straight back.
3. **Movement path.** Bar slides down the thighs, staying in contact with the legs.
4. **Breathing.** Inhale and brace before descending.
5. **Contraction.** Hamstrings stretch; drive the hips forward to rise.
6. **End position.** Stand tall, glutes squeezed, no lean-back.
7. **Return.** Repeat without resting at the bottom.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Lower until the hamstrings limit you or the back starts to round — usually just below the knees.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Neutral spine, shins near vertical; knee angle stays almost constant.
- **Tempo:** 3 s down, 1 s up.
- **Muscle engagement:** Range is set by hip movement, not by touching the floor.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Rounding the back to reach lower | Stop when hips stop moving back |
| Squatting the RDL | Keep knees fixed; push hips back |
| Bar drifting forward | Drag it along the thighs |
| Leaning back at the top | Finish with glutes, stand straight |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Learn with dumbbells; 2–3 × 10 | 3–4 × 6–10 | 3–5 × 5–10 | 2–3 min | Add 2.5–5 kg; use straps when grip limits |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | High | Fair (hinge skill) | Moderate | Good | Safe with neutral spine |

- **Advantages:** Loads hamstrings and glutes at long length; strong carryover to deadlifts
- **Disadvantages:** Misses biceps femoris short head; grip and low back can fatigue first

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Seated leg curl + 45° back extension · Dumbbell: Dumbbell RDL · Bodyweight: Single-leg RDL · Easier: Dumbbell RDL to mid-shin · Harder: Deficit RDL, paused RDL

## 5.2 Dumbbell Romanian Deadlift

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · Hip hinge · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings, gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Adductor magnus, erectors, grip
- **Specific muscles:** Hamstring long heads, glute max
- **Joint actions:** Hip extension

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | No | No | No | Hinge with dowel |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing tall, dumbbells in front of thighs.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width.
- **Hands / grip:** Neutral or overhand grip.
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand tall, knees soft.
2. **Initiate.** Hips back.
3. **Movement path.** Dumbbells travel close to the legs.
4. **Breathing.** Brace, inhale down.
5. **Contraction.** Drive hips forward.
6. **End position.** Standing tall.
7. **Return.** Control down.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Mid-shin, spine neutral.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Same as barbell RDL.
- **Tempo:** 3 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Dumbbells allow a more natural hand path and are easier to learn.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Rounding | Shorter range |
| Dumbbells swinging forward | Keep them close |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 10–12 | 3 × 8–12 | 3–4 × 8–12 heavy or single-leg | 90 s–2 min | Heavier dumbbells → barbell |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | Moderate–high | Excellent | Moderate | Excellent | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Easiest way to learn the hinge
- **Disadvantages:** Heavy dumbbells limit loading eventually

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Seated leg curl · Dumbbell: This is the DB option · Bodyweight: Single-leg RDL · Easier: Kettlebell RDL · Harder: Barbell RDL

## 5.3 Stiff-Leg Deadlift (dumbbell or barbell)

**Category:** Compound · Hip hinge · **Level:** Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings
- **Secondary:** Gluteus maximus, erectors
- **Specific muscles:** Hamstring long heads at very long length
- **Joint actions:** Hip extension with near-straight knees; each rep starts from the floor (dead stop)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | Yes | No | No | Single-leg RDL |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Bar or dumbbells on the floor, knees nearly straight, hips high.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width.
- **Hands / grip:** Overhand.
- **Starting range:** Weight on floor.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Hinge down to the weight with nearly straight legs.
2. **Initiate.** Brace, pull the slack out of the bar.
3. **Movement path.** Lift by extending the hips.
4. **Breathing.** Breath held during lift.
5. **Contraction.** Stand tall.
6. **End position.** Standing.
7. **Return.** Return to the floor each rep.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Floor to standing; needs good hamstring flexibility.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Neutral spine — elevate the weight on blocks if you round.
- **Tempo:** Controlled.
- **Muscle engagement:** Straighter knees than an RDL = more hamstring stretch.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Rounding to reach the floor | Elevate the start position |
| Locking knees hard | Keep a micro-bend |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Use RDL first | 3 × 6–10 | 3–4 × 5–8 | 2–3 min | Load slowly |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | High | Fair | Moderate | Good | Moderate — needs flexibility |

- **Advantages:** Maximal hamstring stretch under load
- **Disadvantages:** Low back limits it more than an RDL

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Seated leg curl · Dumbbell: Dumbbell SLDL · Bodyweight: Single-leg RDL · Easier: RDL · Harder: Deficit SLDL

**I. Visual learning**

| Item | Resource |
|---|---|
| Best tutorial (verified) | [FIX Your RDL Form! (Ultimate Romanian Deadlift Tutorial)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5bJEigM5iVg) — *Squat University* |
| Video type / learning focus | Hinge tutorial (closely related). Hip hinge mechanics; the SLDL differs mainly in starting from the floor with straighter knees |

## 5.4 Single-Leg Romanian Deadlift

**Category:** Unilateral · Dumbbell/Kettlebell · Functional · **Level:** Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings, gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Gluteus medius, foot and ankle stabilisers, adductors
- **Specific muscles:** Hamstring long heads, glute max, glute medius (pelvic control), fibularis and foot intrinsics (balance)
- **Joint actions:** Hip extension on one leg; ankle/foot stabilisation

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | No | Alternative | No | Bodyweight SL-RDL |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing on one leg, soft knee.
- **Foot position:** Stance foot gripping the floor (tripod: big toe, little toe, heel).
- **Hands / grip:** Dumbbell in the opposite hand (contralateral).
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Balance on one leg.
2. **Initiate.** Hinge at the hip while the free leg extends back.
3. **Movement path.** Torso and back leg move as one straight line.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe steadily.
5. **Contraction.** Feel the stance-leg hamstring and glute stretch; drive back up.
6. **End position.** Stand tall on one leg.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Until torso is near parallel or balance/hamstrings limit.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Hips square to the floor (don't open the hip of the back leg).
- **Tempo:** 3 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Doubles as a balance and foot-control drill.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Hip rotating open | Point back toes to the floor |
| Losing balance | Touch a wall lightly; kickstand variation |
| Rounding | Shorter range |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 8 / leg bodyweight | 3 × 8–10 / leg | 3–4 × 6–10 / leg heavy | 60–90 s | Bodyweight → kickstand → loaded |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Fair (balance) | High | Excellent | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Combines hamstring loading with balance and hip stability — useful for runners
- **Disadvantages:** Balance limits load

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Cable single-leg RDL · Dumbbell: Kettlebell SL-RDL · Bodyweight: Bodyweight SL-RDL · Easier: Kickstand RDL · Harder: Heavier, deficit or barbell version

**I. Visual learning**

## 5.5 Seated Leg Curl

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Beginner → Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings (all four including biceps femoris short head)
- **Secondary:** Gastrocnemius
- **Specific muscles:** Semitendinosus, semimembranosus, biceps femoris long and short heads
- **Joint actions:** Knee flexion with the hip flexed (hamstrings lengthened)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Seated leg curl machine | No | No | No | Alternative | Nordic curl / sliding leg curl |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated, leaning slightly forward to increase hip flexion (more hamstring stretch).
- **Foot position:** Lower pad behind the ankles; thigh pad snug.
- **Hands / grip:** On handles.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Knee aligned with pivot; thigh pad locks thighs down firmly.
- **Starting range:** Legs extended.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Adjust pads and pivot.
2. **Initiate.** Curl by pulling heels down and back.
3. **Movement path.** Smooth arc to full flexion.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale as you curl.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze 1 s.
6. **End position.** Heels under the seat.
7. **Return.** Return slowly to full extension.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range including the stretched position.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Hips stay down.
- **Tempo:** 1 s curl, 1 s squeeze, 3 s return.
- **Muscle engagement:** Seated curls produced greater hamstring growth than lying curls over 12 weeks (Maeo et al., 2021).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Thigh pad loose | Tighten it |
| Cutting the stretch short | Return fully |
| Momentum | Slow eccentric |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 10–15 | 3–4 × 8–12 | 3–5 × 6–15, lengthened partials | 90 s | Reps then plates |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | Moderate | Excellent | Very low | Good | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Best-evidenced hamstring isolation; trains the short head that hinges miss
- **Disadvantages:** Machine-dependent

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Lying leg curl · Dumbbell: Dumbbell lying curl · Bodyweight: Nordic / slider curl · Easier: Single-leg light · Harder: Slow eccentrics, partials at long length

**I. Visual learning**

| Item | Resource |
|---|---|
| Best tutorial (verified) | [How to Use Leg Curl Machines (Prone & Seated) | Proper Setup & Form](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=S367qaHeYWU) — *Fitness 19* |
| Video type / learning focus | Machine setup tutorial. Pivot alignment and pad setup on seated and prone machines |
| Also useful (verified) | [Science-Based Hamstrings Tips | Weird Hamstring & Shoulder Workout](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kXnRqoW6Alw) — *Jeff Nippard*: Why seated curls and hinges are both needed |
| Most suitable educators | Jeff Nippard, Jeremy Ethier |

## 5.6 Lying Leg Curl

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings
- **Secondary:** Gastrocnemius
- **Specific muscles:** All four hamstrings, biceps femoris short head
- **Joint actions:** Knee flexion with the hip extended (hamstrings shorter)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Lying (prone) leg curl machine | Alternative (between feet) | No | No | Alternative | Slider leg curl |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Prone, hips pressed into the pad.
- **Foot position:** Pad just above heels.
- **Hands / grip:** On handles.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Knees just off the pad edge, aligned to pivot.
- **Starting range:** Legs straight.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Lie prone with knees just off the pad edge and the ankle pad just above the heels.
2. **Initiate.** Press the hips into the pad, brace the trunk, then curl by pulling the heels toward the glutes.
3. **Movement path.** Move in a smooth arc around the knee; the thighs stay on the pad and the hips do not pike up.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale as you curl, inhale as you lower.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze the hamstrings hard for 1 s at the top; keeping the feet dorsiflexed (toes toward shins) lets the gastrocnemius help, pointing them reduces its help.
6. **End position.** Heels close to the glutes, knees fully bent.
7. **Return.** Lower over 3 s to full knee extension without letting the stack touch down.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full extension to full flexion.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Hips stay on pad (not piking).
- **Tempo:** 1 s curl, 1 s squeeze, 3 s lowering.
- **Muscle engagement:** Good second choice when no seated curl is available.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Hips lifting off the pad | Reduce load; press hips down; some machines have an angled pad for this |
| Partial range at the bottom | Return to full knee extension every rep |
| Kicking the weight up | Slow, controlled 1-second curl |
| Pad on the calves | Move pad down to just above the heels |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 10–15 | 3 × 8–12 | 3–4 × 8–15 | 90 s | Reps then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Excellent | Very low | Good | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Easy, effective knee-flexion work
- **Disadvantages:** Less hamstring stretch than seated version

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Seated leg curl · Dumbbell: DB lying curl · Bodyweight: Slider curl · Easier: Single leg · Harder: Slow eccentric

**I. Visual learning**

|---|---|

## 5.7 Nordic Hamstring Curl

**Category:** Bodyweight · Eccentric · Injury-prevention · **Level:** Intermediate → Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings (eccentric)
- **Secondary:** Glutes, core (isometric)
- **Specific muscles:** Semitendinosus and biceps femoris (long and short heads)
- **Joint actions:** Eccentric knee flexion control with the hip extended

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Optional | Nordic bench or GHD (optional) | No | No | No | Assistance band | Is bodyweight |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Kneeling tall on a pad, body straight knees-to-head.
- **Foot position:** Ankles anchored under a pad, strap or partner.
- **Hands / grip:** In front, ready to catch.
- **Starting range:** Upright.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Kneel tall, squeeze glutes.
2. **Initiate.** Lean forward slowly as one plank.
3. **Movement path.** Resist with the hamstrings as long as possible.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe out slowly.
5. **Contraction.** Hamstrings work eccentrically.
6. **End position.** Catch yourself with the hands.
7. **Return.** Push off lightly and pull back up (or use hands to return).
8. **Recommended ROM.** Lower as far as you can control.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Hips extended throughout.
- **Tempo:** 3–5 s lowering.
- **Muscle engagement:** Programs including Nordics roughly halved hamstring injury rates in a meta-analysis (van Dyk et al., 2019), although a methodological reappraisal judged the evidence less conclusive (Impellizzeri et al., 2021).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bending at the hips | Squeeze glutes |
| Dropping fast | Use a band or shorter range |
| Too much volume early | Start with 2 × 4–5; expect soreness |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Band-assisted 2 × 4–6 | 3 × 5–8 | 3 × 6–10, weighted | 2 min | Less assistance → slower → load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | High (eccentric) | Poor (very hard) | Low | Excellent | Very sore initially; build gradually |

- **Advantages:** Very strong eccentric hamstring stimulus; injury-prevention evidence
- **Disadvantages:** Hard to scale; heavy soreness

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Seated leg curl (slow eccentric) · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: This is the BW option · Easier: Band-assisted or partial Nordic · Harder: Weighted Nordic

## 5.8 Glute-Ham Raise (GHR)

**Category:** Machine · Bodyweight · Posterior chain · **Level:** Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Hamstrings
- **Secondary:** Gluteus maximus, gastrocnemius
- **Specific muscles:** All hamstrings, gastrocnemius (feet pressing the plate)
- **Joint actions:** Knee flexion and hip extension

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Glute-ham developer (GHD) | No | No | No | Assistance | Nordic curl |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Knees just behind the pad, body horizontal or upright.
- **Foot position:** Pressing the footplate, ankles locked under rollers.
- **Hands / grip:** Crossed or behind head.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Footplate distance so knees sit just behind the pad.
- **Starting range:** Torso horizontal/upright.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Lock the ankles under the rollers with the knees just behind the thigh pad, torso horizontal.
2. **Initiate.** Press the balls of the feet into the footplate and squeeze the glutes.
3. **Movement path.** Curl the body up by flexing the knees, keeping a straight line from knees to head.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale on the way up.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze the hamstrings as the torso reaches vertical.
6. **End position.** Kneeling upright on the pad.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly (3 s) to horizontal, then optionally hinge down into a back extension.
8. **Recommended ROM.** From horizontal to upright (knee flexion), with optional hip hinge.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Straight line from knees to head.
- **Tempo:** 1–2 s up, 3 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Combines hip extension and knee flexion.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Using hips only (back extension) | Keep body straight and curl |
| Pad position wrong | Knees behind pad |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Assisted 2 × 5 | 3 × 6–8 | 3–4 × 8–12 weighted | 2 min | Less assistance → load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | High | Poor | Low | Low (specialised) | Safe with control |

- **Advantages:** Combines both hamstring functions
- **Disadvantages:** Hard; needs a GHD

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Seated leg curl · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: Nordic curl · Easier: Band-assisted · Harder: Weighted GHR

## 5.9 Conventional Deadlift

**Category:** Compound · Barbell · Posterior chain · **Level:** Intermediate → Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Gluteus maximus, hamstrings, quadriceps, erector spinae
- **Secondary:** Adductor magnus, traps, lats, grip
- **Specific muscles:** Glute max, adductor magnus, hamstrings, vasti (off the floor), erectors (isometric)
- **Joint actions:** Hip and knee extension from the floor

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | No | Yes | No | No | Kettlebell deadlift |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Bar over mid-foot, shins touching bar, hips between knees and shoulders, chest up.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width, toes slightly out.
- **Hands / grip:** Just outside shins; double overhand, mixed or hook grip.
- **Starting range:** Bar on floor.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Approach, bar over mid-foot.
2. **Initiate.** Grip, pull slack out of the bar, brace.
3. **Movement path.** Push the floor away; bar travels in a straight vertical line up the shins and thighs.
4. **Breathing.** Hold breath during the pull.
5. **Contraction.** Hips and shoulders rise together; finish with glutes.
6. **End position.** Stand tall, shoulders behind bar slightly.
7. **Return.** Lower by pushing hips back then bending knees once past them.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Floor to lockout.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Neutral spine; bar close.
- **Tempo:** Controlled; reset each rep for beginners.
- **Muscle engagement:** More of a strength and posterior-chain exercise than a leg-hypertrophy exercise.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bar away from legs | Drag it up the shins |
| Rounding lower back | Lighter load, brace harder |
| Jerking the bar | Pull slack out first |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Learn with kettlebell or trap bar; 3 × 5 | 3 × 3–6 | 3–5 × 1–5 | 3–4 min | Small load jumps |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate | Very high | Fair | Moderate | Good | Safe with technique; high fatigue |

- **Advantages:** Builds whole-body strength
- **Disadvantages:** High systemic fatigue per unit of leg growth

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Trap-bar deadlift · Dumbbell: Dumbbell deadlift · Bodyweight: Kettlebell deadlift · Easier: Trap bar from high handles · Harder: Deficit or paused deadlift

# 6. Adductor and abductor exercise library

The adductors are the largest muscle group on the inside of the thigh, and the adductor magnus is one of the strongest hip extensors in a deep squat. Abductor (glute medius/minimus) entries are in Chapter 3: **3.4 Seated hip abduction**, **3.5 Side-lying hip abduction** and **3.6 Banded lateral walk**.

| # | Exercise | Category | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | Seated Hip Adduction (machine) | Isolation · Machine | Beginner |
| 6.2 | Copenhagen Plank / Copenhagen Adduction | Bodyweight · Stability · Injury-prevention | Beginner (short lever) → Advanced |
| 6.3 | Sumo Squat (dumbbell or kettlebell) | Compound · Dumbbell | Beginner |
| 6.4 | Standing Cable Hip Adduction | Isolation · Cable · Unilateral | Beginner |

## 6.1 Seated Hip Adduction (machine)

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Adductors
- **Secondary:** Gracilis, pectineus
- **Specific muscles:** Adductor longus, brevis, magnus, gracilis, pectineus
- **Joint actions:** Hip adduction (hip flexed)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Hip adduction machine (inner thigh) | No | No | Alternative | Alternative | Copenhagen plank / side-lying adduction |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated tall.
- **Foot position:** On foot rests.
- **Hands / grip:** On handles.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Pads inside the knees; start wide enough for a stretch.
- **Starting range:** Legs apart.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Sit tall, pads inside knees.
2. **Initiate.** Squeeze the thighs together.
3. **Movement path.** Smooth arc inward.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale in.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze 1 s.
6. **End position.** Thighs together.
7. **Return.** Return slowly to the stretch.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range from wide to closed.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Stay seated.
- **Tempo:** 1 s squeeze in, 1 s hold, 3 s return to the stretch.
- **Muscle engagement:** The wide (stretched) start is where most hypertrophy stimulus is likely to come from (long-length principle, inference).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Too wide start causing groin pain | Reduce range initially |
| Slamming the stack | Control |
| Leaning forward and using momentum | Sit tall, grip the handles |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 12–15 | 3 × 10–15 | 3–4 × 10–20 | 60 s | Reps then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate | Low–moderate | Excellent | Very low | Good | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Direct adductor loading
- **Disadvantages:** Seated only; frontal-plane only

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Cable adduction · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: Copenhagen plank · Easier: Small range · Harder: Slow eccentric

|---|---|
| Best tutorial | No direct video verified in this research; use the search link (prefer E3 Rehab, Squat University, Jeff Nippard or a physiotherapist channel in the results) |

## 6.2 Copenhagen Plank / Copenhagen Adduction

**Category:** Bodyweight · Stability · Injury-prevention · **Level:** Beginner (short lever) → Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Adductors (top leg)
- **Secondary:** Obliques, gluteus medius (bottom leg), quadratus lumborum
- **Specific muscles:** Adductor longus and magnus, gracilis
- **Joint actions:** Isometric/eccentric hip adduction with lateral trunk stability

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | No | No | No | No | Is bodyweight (bench required) |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Side plank on the elbow.
- **Foot position:** Top leg on a bench (knee for short lever, ankle for long lever); bottom leg below the bench.
- **Hands / grip:** Elbow under shoulder.
- **Starting range:** Hips on the floor.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Set the top leg on the bench.
2. **Initiate.** Press the top leg into the bench.
3. **Movement path.** Lift the hips into a straight side-plank line.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe steadily.
5. **Contraction.** Hold (or bring the bottom leg up to the bench for the dynamic version).
6. **End position.** Straight line ankle–hip–shoulder.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Hold 10–30 s or 6–10 reps.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Hips stacked, no sagging.
- **Tempo:** Holds of 10–30 s, or dynamic reps of 2 s up / 2 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** A cluster RCT in footballers found the Copenhagen programme reduced groin problems by about 41% (Harøy et al., 2019).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Hips sagging | Shorter lever, shorter holds |
| Starting with long lever | Begin with the knee on the bench |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Short lever 2 × 15–20 s/side | 3 × 20–30 s or 6–8 reps | Long lever 3 × 8–12 reps | 60 s | Short → long lever → dynamic → longer holds |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low–moderate | Moderate (adductor) | Good (short lever) | Moderate | Excellent | Safe if progressed |

- **Advantages:** Best-evidenced adductor injury-prevention exercise
- **Disadvantages:** Groin discomfort when progressed too fast

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Adduction machine · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: This is the BW option · Easier: Short lever on knee · Harder: Long-lever dynamic

## 6.3 Sumo Squat (dumbbell or kettlebell)

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Adductors, gluteus maximus, quadriceps
- **Secondary:** Hamstrings
- **Specific muscles:** Adductor magnus and longus, glute max, vasti
- **Joint actions:** Hip extension, adduction from an abducted position, knee extension

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | Optional | No | No | Wide-stance bodyweight squat |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Upright, dumbbell held vertically between legs.
- **Foot position:** Wide stance, toes out ~30–45°.
- **Hands / grip:** Both hands on one dumbbell head.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Stand on two boxes for extra depth (deficit).
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Wide stance, weight hanging.
2. **Initiate.** Sit down, knees pushing out over toes.
3. **Movement path.** Torso upright.
4. **Breathing.** Inhale down.
5. **Contraction.** Drive up squeezing inner thighs and glutes.
6. **End position.** Stand tall.
7. **Return.** Control down.
8. **Recommended ROM.** As deep as hips allow.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knees follow toes.
- **Tempo:** 3 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** A wide stance increases the adductor stretch and demand.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Knees caving | Push knees out |
| Toes forward with wide stance | Turn toes out |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 12 | 3 × 10–15 | 3–4 × 8–12 deficit | 90 s | Heavier → deficit |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate | Moderate | Excellent | Low | Excellent | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Easy adductor and glute loading
- **Disadvantages:** Load limited to one dumbbell

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Adduction machine / wide leg press · Dumbbell: This is the DB option · Bodyweight: Wide bodyweight squat · Easier: Shallow sumo · Harder: Deficit sumo, barbell sumo squat

## 6.4 Standing Cable Hip Adduction

**Category:** Isolation · Cable · Unilateral · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Adductors
- **Secondary:** Gluteus medius (stance leg)
- **Specific muscles:** Adductor longus, brevis, gracilis, pectineus
- **Joint actions:** Hip adduction (hip extended — standing)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Low cable pulley with ankle cuff | No | No | Yes | Alternative | Side-lying adduction |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing side-on to the pulley, working leg nearest it.
- **Foot position:** Cuff on inside ankle.
- **Hands / grip:** Hold the frame.
- **Starting range:** Working leg out to the side.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand tall.
2. **Initiate.** Pull the leg across the body.
3. **Movement path.** Smooth arc to midline and slightly past.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale in.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze.
6. **End position.** Leg crossing slightly in front of the stance leg.
7. **Return.** Return slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full arc.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Torso still.
- **Tempo:** 1 s in, 1 s squeeze, 2 s out.
- **Muscle engagement:** Trains adductors in a standing (hip-extended) position, complementing the seated machine.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Leaning | Lighter |
| Bending the stance knee and dipping | Stand tall on a soft, stable stance knee |
| Using too much range past midline with trunk lean | Stop just past midline |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 12–15/leg | 3 × 12–15 | 3 × 15–20 | 60 s | Reps then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low–moderate | Low | Excellent | Moderate | Good | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Standing adductor work
- **Disadvantages:** Small loads

**H. Alternatives**

# 7. Knee strength and stability

No single muscle 'stabilises the knee'. Knee control comes from the **quadriceps and hamstrings** (co-contraction), the **gastrocnemius** and **popliteus** (rotational control and unlocking from full extension), and the **glute medius and external rotators** above it, which limit inward knee collapse. Practical knee strength therefore means: full-range quad work (Chapter 4), hamstring curls (Chapter 5), hip abduction (Chapter 3) and the single-leg patterns below. The 'VMO' cannot be isolated by foot position or short-range work; it grows with any heavy quad training. For irritable knees, the Spanish squat (4.10) and leg extension with a controlled range (4.6) are well-tolerated options.

| # | Exercise | Category | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | Reverse Lunge | Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral | Beginner |
| 7.2 | Walking Lunge | Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral · Functional | Intermediate |
| 7.3 | Forward Lunge | Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral | Intermediate |
| 7.4 | Terminal Knee Extension (TKE) | Resistance band · Rehab-oriented | Beginner |

## 7.1 Reverse Lunge

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps, gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Adductors, hamstrings, gluteus medius
- **Specific muscles:** Vasti, glute max (front leg)
- **Joint actions:** Knee and hip extension on the front leg

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Optional | Smith machine (optional) | Yes | Optional | No | No | Bodyweight reverse lunge |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing tall, dumbbells at sides.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width.
- **Hands / grip:** Dumbbells at sides.
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand tall.
2. **Initiate.** Step one foot back.
3. **Movement path.** Lower until the back knee is just above the floor.
4. **Breathing.** Inhale down.
5. **Contraction.** Drive through the front heel/mid-foot to return.
6. **End position.** Feet together, standing.
7. **Return.** Alternate or repeat one side.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Back knee near floor; front thigh ~parallel.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Front knee tracks toes; torso upright or slight lean.
- **Tempo:** 2 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Stepping back keeps the load on the front leg and is gentler on the knee than a forward lunge.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Stepping too narrow (tightrope) | Keep feet hip-width |
| Pushing off the back foot | Front leg does the work |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 8 / leg | 3 × 8–12 / leg | 3–4 × 6–10 / leg heavy or deficit | 90 s | Load → deficit |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Excellent | Moderate | Excellent | Knee-friendly |

- **Advantages:** The most beginner-friendly lunge
- **Disadvantages:** Balance can limit load

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Smith reverse lunge · Dumbbell: This is DB · Bodyweight: Bodyweight · Easier: Assisted (hold a rail) · Harder: Deficit reverse lunge

## 7.2 Walking Lunge

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral · Functional · **Level:** Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps, gluteus maximus
- **Secondary:** Adductors, hamstrings, calves, glute medius
- **Specific muscles:** Vasti, glute max
- **Joint actions:** Knee and hip extension; deceleration on landing

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | Optional | No | No | Bodyweight walking lunge |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing, dumbbells at sides.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width, clear walking space.
- **Hands / grip:** Dumbbells at sides.
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand tall.
2. **Initiate.** Step forward into a lunge.
3. **Movement path.** Lower until back knee nearly touches.
4. **Breathing.** Inhale down.
5. **Contraction.** Drive up and bring the back foot through into the next step.
6. **End position.** Upright between steps.
7. **Return.** Continue walking.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full depth each step.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knee tracks toe; stride long enough to keep the heel down.
- **Tempo:** Controlled, not rushed.
- **Muscle engagement:** Longer strides bias glutes; shorter strides bias quads.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Short choppy steps | Lengthen stride |
| Knee slamming into floor | Control depth |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 8 / leg | 3 × 10–12 / leg | 3–4 × 10–20 steps heavy | 90–120 s | Load, distance |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | Moderate | Good | High | Good (needs space) | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Very effective, functional glute/quad builder
- **Disadvantages:** Fatiguing; needs floor space

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg press · Dumbbell: This is DB · Bodyweight: Bodyweight · Easier: Reverse lunge · Harder: Barbell walking lunge

## 7.3 Forward Lunge

**Category:** Compound · Dumbbell · Unilateral · **Level:** Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps
- **Secondary:** Gluteus maximus, adductors
- **Specific muscles:** Vasti (eccentric braking emphasis)
- **Joint actions:** Knee and hip extension after decelerating forward

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes | Optional | No | No | Bodyweight forward lunge |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing tall.
- **Foot position:** Hip-width.
- **Hands / grip:** Dumbbells at sides.
- **Starting range:** Standing.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand tall.
2. **Initiate.** Step forward.
3. **Movement path.** Absorb the landing and lower straight down.
4. **Breathing.** Inhale down.
5. **Contraction.** Push back to the start with the front leg.
6. **End position.** Standing.
7. **Return.** Alternate.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Back knee near floor.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Front knee over toes.
- **Tempo:** Controlled landing.
- **Muscle engagement:** More knee-braking (deceleration) than a reverse lunge.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Landing heavy on the heel and falling forward | Shorter step, control |
| Torso collapsing | Chest up |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Learn reverse first | 3 × 8–10 / leg | 3 × 8–12 / leg | 90 s | Load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Fair | High | Excellent | More knee stress than reverse lunge |

- **Advantages:** Trains deceleration (useful for sport)
- **Disadvantages:** Harder on sensitive knees

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg press · Dumbbell: DB · Bodyweight: BW · Easier: Reverse lunge · Harder: Deficit forward lunge

## 7.4 Terminal Knee Extension (TKE)

**Category:** Resistance band · Rehab-oriented · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Quadriceps (end-range knee extension)
- **Secondary:** Popliteus, gastrocnemius (co-contraction)
- **Specific muscles:** Vasti near full extension
- **Joint actions:** Knee extension from ~30° to full extension against a band pulling the knee forward

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | No | No | Alternative | Yes (anchored behind the knee) | Quad set (isometric) |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing facing the anchor, band behind the knee.
- **Foot position:** Working foot flat, slight knee bend.
- **Hands / grip:** Free.
- **Starting range:** Knee ~30° bent.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Band behind knee.
2. **Initiate.** Straighten the knee fully against the band.
3. **Movement path.** Short arc.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze the quad 2 s.
6. **End position.** Knee fully straight (not hyperextended).
7. **Return.** Release slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** ~30° to 0°.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Hip, knee and ankle aligned.
- **Tempo:** Slow with holds.
- **Muscle engagement:** Rehab tool for regaining full knee extension after injury.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Hyperextending | Stop at straight |
| Hip moving instead of the knee | Keep hips still; only the knee straightens |
| Band too light to feel the quad | Use a heavier band or step further back |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 15 | 3 × 15–20 | Warm-up use | 30 s | Heavier band |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low | Low | Excellent | Low | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Restores end-range knee control
- **Disadvantages:** Not a size builder

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg extension (short range) · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: Quad set · Easier: Light band · Harder: Single-leg stance TKE

# 8. Calf exercise library

The gastrocnemius crosses the knee, so it is best trained with a **straight knee**; the soleus is best emphasised with a **bent knee**. For both, the bottom stretch is the most productive part of the rep — pause there instead of bouncing.

| # | Exercise | Category | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8.1 | Standing Calf Raise (machine or Smith) | Isolation · Machine | Beginner → Advanced |
| 8.2 | Seated Calf Raise | Isolation · Machine | Beginner |
| 8.3 | Donkey Calf Raise | Isolation · Machine | Intermediate |
| 8.4 | Single-Leg Calf Raise (dumbbell, on a step) | Unilateral · Dumbbell/Bodyweight | Beginner → Intermediate |
| 8.5 | Leg-Press Calf Raise | Isolation · Machine | Beginner |

## 8.1 Standing Calf Raise (machine or Smith)

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Beginner → Advanced

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Gastrocnemius (both heads)
- **Secondary:** Soleus, deep plantarflexors
- **Specific muscles:** Medial and lateral gastrocnemius, soleus, FHL, tibialis posterior, fibularis
- **Joint actions:** Ankle plantarflexion with straight knee

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Standing calf raise machine (shoulder pads) or Smith machine + step | Alternative | No | No | No | Single-leg calf raise on a step |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing tall under the pads, knees straight but not locked.
- **Foot position:** Balls of the feet on the platform edge, hip-width, toes forward.
- **Hands / grip:** On handles.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Shoulder pad height so you start at a deep stretch.
- **Starting range:** Heels as low as possible (full stretch).

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Set feet; heels hanging.
2. **Initiate.** Push through the big-toe side of the forefoot.
3. **Movement path.** Rise straight up.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale up.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze at the top 1 s.
6. **End position.** Highest heel position.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly 2–3 s and **pause 1–2 s in the bottom stretch**.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range; the bottom stretch matters most.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Ankles don't roll outward.
- **Tempo:** 1 s up, 1 s squeeze, 3 s down, 2 s stretch pause.
- **Muscle engagement:** Partials in the stretched half grew the gastrocnemius more than partials in the top half (Kassiano et al., 2023).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bouncing out of the bottom (Achilles 'spring') | Pause in the stretch |
| Bending the knees | Keep knees straight for gastrocnemius |
| Tiny range with heavy load | Full range first |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 12–15 | 3–4 × 10–15 | 4–5 × 8–20, lengthened partials | 60–90 s | Reps then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High (gastrocnemius) | Moderate | Excellent | Low | Good | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Direct gastrocnemius loading through full range
- **Disadvantages:** Machine pads can load the spine heavily

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg-press calf raise · Dumbbell: Single-leg dumbbell calf raise · Bodyweight: Single-leg BW calf raise · Easier: Two-leg BW · Harder: Donkey calf raise, slow eccentrics

## 8.2 Seated Calf Raise

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Soleus
- **Secondary:** Gastrocnemius (less, shortened at the knee)
- **Specific muscles:** Soleus, FHL, tibialis posterior
- **Joint actions:** Ankle plantarflexion with the knee bent ~90°

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Seated calf raise machine | Alternative (on knees) | No | No | No | Bent-knee wall calf raise |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated, knees under pad.
- **Foot position:** Balls of the feet on the plate.
- **Hands / grip:** On the lever/handles.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Pad snug over lower thighs.
- **Starting range:** Heels down, full stretch.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Release the safety.
2. **Initiate.** Push up through the forefoot.
3. **Movement path.** Rise fully.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale up.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze.
6. **End position.** Top.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly with a stretch pause.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Feet straight.
- **Tempo:** 1-1-3-2.
- **Muscle engagement:** Bent knee slackens gastrocnemius, so the soleus does more of the work.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bouncing | Pause in stretch |
| Short range | Full stretch |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 12–20 | 3–4 × 12–20 | 4 × 10–25 | 60 s | Reps then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high (soleus) | Moderate | Excellent | Very low | Good | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Only practical way to emphasise the soleus
- **Disadvantages:** Machine-dependent

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg-press with knees bent · Dumbbell: DB on knees seated · Bodyweight: Bent-knee wall raise · Easier: Light · Harder: Slow eccentrics, pauses

## 8.3 Donkey Calf Raise

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Gastrocnemius
- **Secondary:** Soleus
- **Specific muscles:** Gastrocnemius in a lengthened position (hips flexed don't affect it; straight knee + deep dorsiflexion does)
- **Joint actions:** Plantarflexion, straight knee, torso hinged

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | Donkey calf raise machine (or leg press / Smith alternative) | No | No | No | No | Single-leg calf raise |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Hinged at hips, back under the pad, hands on support.
- **Foot position:** Balls of feet on platform.
- **Hands / grip:** On handles.
- **Starting range:** Heels down.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Hinge at the hips under the pad, back flat, hands on the handles.
2. **Initiate.** Push up through the big-toe side of the forefoot.
3. **Movement path.** Rise straight up without bending the knees.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale up, inhale down.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze 1 s at the top.
6. **End position.** Highest heel position.
7. **Return.** Lower over 3 s and pause 1–2 s in the deepest stretch.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range; the stretched bottom position matters most.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knees straight.
- **Tempo:** 1 s up, 1 s squeeze, 3 s down, 2 s stretch pause.
- **Muscle engagement:** Comfortable deep stretch for many lifters; less spinal compression than standing machines.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bending the knees | Keep legs straight to keep load on the gastrocnemius |
| Bouncing out of the bottom | Pause in the stretch |
| Rounding the back under the pad | Hinge with a flat back; lighter load |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 12–15 (if available) | 3–4 × 10–15 | 4 × 8–20, lengthened partials | 60–90 s | Reps then load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | Moderate | Good | Low | Low (rare machine) | Safe |

- **Advantages:** Excellent stretch
- **Disadvantages:** Rare equipment

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Leg-press calf raise · Dumbbell: SL DB raise · Bodyweight: SL BW raise · Easier: Standing machine · Harder: Slow tempo

## 8.4 Single-Leg Calf Raise (dumbbell, on a step)

**Category:** Unilateral · Dumbbell/Bodyweight · **Level:** Beginner → Intermediate

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Gastrocnemius, soleus
- **Secondary:** Fibularis, tibialis posterior, foot intrinsics
- **Specific muscles:** Both calf muscles plus ankle stabilisers
- **Joint actions:** Plantarflexion with balance demand

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Yes (optional) | No | No | No | Is bodyweight |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing on one foot on a step, holding a rail.
- **Foot position:** Ball of foot on the edge.
- **Hands / grip:** One hand on the rail, dumbbell in the other.
- **Starting range:** Heel low.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Stand on one foot.
2. **Initiate.** Rise through the big toe.
3. **Movement path.** Straight up, ankle not rolling out.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze.
6. **End position.** Top.
7. **Return.** Lower with a stretch pause.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Ankle stays in line (no outward roll).
- **Tempo:** 1-1-3-2.
- **Muscle engagement:** Clinicians often use this as a calf-endurance test — healthy adults commonly manage around 20–25 reps.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Rolling to little toe | Push through big toe |
| Bouncing | Pause |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 10–15 / leg BW | 3 × 12–15 / leg with DB | 4 × 10–20 heavy | 45–60 s | BW → DB → heavier |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moderate–high | Moderate | Excellent | Moderate | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Needs only a step; builds ankle stability too
- **Disadvantages:** Load limited by holding a dumbbell

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Calf machine · Dumbbell: This is DB · Bodyweight: This is BW · Easier: Two-leg · Harder: Slow-eccentric, weighted

## 8.5 Leg-Press Calf Raise

**Category:** Isolation · Machine · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Gastrocnemius
- **Secondary:** Soleus
- **Specific muscles:** Gastrocnemius (straight knee)
- **Joint actions:** Plantarflexion

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yes | 45° or horizontal leg press | No | No | No | No | Single-leg calf raise |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated in leg press, legs straight (not locked).
- **Foot position:** Balls of feet on the bottom edge of the platform.
- **Hands / grip:** On handles; **keep the safety catches engaged where the machine allows**.
- **Starting range:** Toes pulled toward shins (stretch).

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Set feet on the edge.
2. **Initiate.** Push the platform with the forefoot.
3. **Movement path.** Point the toes fully.
4. **Breathing.** Exhale.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze.
6. **End position.** Full plantarflexion.
7. **Return.** Return to stretch slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knees straight.
- **Tempo:** 1-1-3-2.
- **Muscle engagement:** No spinal loading; easy to load heavily.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Feet slipping off the platform | Keep forefoot well on; use safeties |
| Bending knees | Straight legs |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2–3 × 15 | 3–4 × 10–15 | 4 × 8–20 | 60 s | Load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High | Moderate | Excellent | Very low | Excellent | Safe if feet stay on |

- **Advantages:** Heavy calf loading without spinal compression
- **Disadvantages:** Foot-slip risk

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Standing calf machine · Dumbbell: SL DB · Bodyweight: SL BW · Easier: Light · Harder: Slow tempo

# 9. Shin and lower-leg exercise library

The front of the shin (tibialis anterior and the toe extensors) controls the foot during every step, and the deep posterior muscle (tibialis posterior) supports the arch. Neither is trained by conventional leg exercises.

| # | Exercise | Category | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9.1 | Tibialis Raise | Bodyweight / Machine / Band · Isolation | Beginner |
| 9.2 | Seated Banded Dorsiflexion | Resistance band · Isolation · Rehab-oriented | Beginner |
| 9.3 | Tibialis Posterior Heel Raise (ball squeeze) | Bodyweight · Isolation · Rehab-oriented | Beginner |

## 9.1 Tibialis Raise

*Also called: toe raise, reverse calf raise, shin raise.* 
**Category:** Bodyweight / Machine / Band · Isolation · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Tibialis anterior
- **Secondary:** EHL, EDL, fibularis tertius
- **Specific muscles:** Tibialis anterior, extensor hallucis longus, extensor digitorum longus
- **Joint actions:** Ankle dorsiflexion

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Optional | Tibialis-raise machine / tib bar (optional) | Optional (between feet, seated) | No | No | Alternative | Wall tibialis raise |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Back and hips against a wall, feet ~30–60 cm in front.
- **Foot position:** Heels on floor, hip-width.
- **Hands / grip:** At sides.
- **Machine / setup adjustments:** Further feet = harder.
- **Starting range:** Toes on the floor.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Lean back on the wall.
2. **Initiate.** Pull the toes and forefoot up toward the shins.
3. **Movement path.** Lift as high as possible, heels stay down.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe steadily.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze the front of the shin 1 s.
6. **End position.** Maximum dorsiflexion.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knees straight.
- **Tempo:** 1 s up, 1 s squeeze, 2 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Common accessory for shin resilience and running; evidence for injury prevention is limited (coaching consensus).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Bending knees | Keep legs straight |
| Partial lift | Full range |
| Too close to wall | Step feet out for load |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 15–20 | 3 × 20–25 | 3 × 15–25 loaded (tib bar) | 45–60 s | Feet further out → single leg → tib bar/machine |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low–moderate | Low–moderate | Excellent | Low | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Trains a muscle almost no leg day trains; simple
- **Disadvantages:** Small load capacity without a tib bar

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Tibialis machine · Dumbbell: Seated DB between feet · Bodyweight: Wall raise · Easier: Feet close to wall · Harder: Single-leg or tib bar

## 9.2 Seated Banded Dorsiflexion

**Category:** Resistance band · Isolation · Rehab-oriented · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Tibialis anterior
- **Secondary:** EHL, EDL
- **Specific muscles:** Tibialis anterior
- **Joint actions:** Dorsiflexion against band resistance

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | No | No | No | Yes (anchored in front) | Tibialis raise |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated on floor, leg straight.
- **Foot position:** Band looped over the top of the forefoot, anchored in front.
- **Hands / grip:** Behind for support.
- **Starting range:** Toes pointed away.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Sit tall.
2. **Initiate.** Pull the foot toward the shin.
3. **Movement path.** Full dorsiflexion.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe.
5. **Contraction.** Squeeze 1–2 s.
6. **End position.** Toes toward you.
7. **Return.** Release slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Foot straight (don't twist).
- **Tempo:** 1 s up, 2 s hold, 2 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Good when standing raises are too easy to do with momentum.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Turning the foot in/out | Keep foot straight |
| Moving the whole leg | Only the ankle moves; keep the knee straight and still |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 15 | 3 × 15–20 | Use heavier band | 30–45 s | Heavier band |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low | Low | Excellent | Low | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Precise and rehab-friendly
- **Disadvantages:** Low load

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: Tib machine · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: Wall raise · Easier: Light band · Harder: Heavy band, pauses

## 9.3 Tibialis Posterior Heel Raise (ball squeeze)

**Category:** Bodyweight · Isolation · Rehab-oriented · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Tibialis posterior
- **Secondary:** Gastrocnemius, soleus, FHL, FDL
- **Specific muscles:** Tibialis posterior (medial arch support)
- **Joint actions:** Plantarflexion with inversion

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | Optional | No | No | Alternative (banded inversion) | Is bodyweight |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Standing, holding a support.
- **Foot position:** Small ball (or yoga block) squeezed between the inner heels/ankles.
- **Hands / grip:** On rail or wall.
- **Starting range:** Heels down.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Squeeze the ball between the ankles.
2. **Initiate.** Rise onto the balls of the feet while keeping the squeeze.
3. **Movement path.** Heels move slightly toward each other (inversion).
4. **Breathing.** Breathe.
5. **Contraction.** Hold at the top 2 s.
6. **End position.** Top position with arches lifted.
7. **Return.** Lower slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Weight toward the big toe; arch lifts.
- **Tempo:** 2 s up, 2 s hold, 3 s down.
- **Muscle engagement:** Used clinically for tibialis posterior tendinopathy and flat-foot control (physiotherapy practice).

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Losing the squeeze | Lighter, slower |
| Rolling out | Push through big toe |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 10–12 | 3 × 12–15 | Single leg with DB | 45 s | Two-leg → single-leg → load |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low | Low | Excellent | Moderate | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Targets arch support muscle directly
- **Disadvantages:** Low hypertrophy value

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: — · Dumbbell: DB single-leg version · Bodyweight: This is BW · Easier: Seated · Harder: Single-leg on a step

# 10. Ankle exercise library

The ankle needs strength in four directions (plantarflexion, dorsiflexion, inversion, eversion), proprioceptive control, and enough dorsiflexion mobility for deep squats and running.

| # | Exercise | Category | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10.1 | Banded Ankle Inversion | Resistance band · Rehab-oriented | Beginner |
| 10.2 | Banded Ankle Eversion | Resistance band · Rehab-oriented | Beginner |
| 10.3 | Single-Leg Balance Progression | Stability · Bodyweight · Rehab-oriented | Beginner → Advanced |
| 10.4 | Knee-to-Wall Ankle Mobility (dorsiflexion) | Mobility | Beginner |

## 10.1 Banded Ankle Inversion

**Category:** Resistance band · Rehab-oriented · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Tibialis posterior
- **Secondary:** Tibialis anterior, FHL, FDL
- **Specific muscles:** Tibialis posterior and anterior
- **Joint actions:** Subtalar inversion (sole turns in)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | No | No | Alternative (light ankle cuff) | Yes (anchored to the outside) | Single-leg balance |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated, leg straight or crossed over the other knee.
- **Foot position:** Band around forefoot, anchored to the OUTSIDE.
- **Hands / grip:** Support.
- **Starting range:** Foot turned out (everted).

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Set band tension.
2. **Initiate.** Turn the sole inward.
3. **Movement path.** Move from the ankle only — don't rotate the leg.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe.
5. **Contraction.** Hold 1 s.
6. **End position.** Sole facing in.
7. **Return.** Return slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full available inversion range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knee still.
- **Tempo:** 1 s turn, 1 s hold, 3 s return.
- **Muscle engagement:** Strengthens medial ankle support.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Rotating the whole leg | Hold the shin still |
| Band slipping toward the toes | Loop band around the mid-foot, not the toes |
| Moving too fast | Slow 3-second return |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 15 | 3 × 15–20 | Heavier band | 30 s | Heavier band |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low | Low | Excellent | Low | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Isolates a weak direction
- **Disadvantages:** Low load

**H. Alternatives**

Machine: — · Dumbbell: — · Bodyweight: Balance work · Easier: Light band · Harder: Heavy band, pauses

## 10.2 Banded Ankle Eversion

**Category:** Resistance band · Rehab-oriented · **Level:** Beginner

**A. Anatomy**

- **Primary:** Fibularis longus and brevis
- **Secondary:** Fibularis tertius, EDL
- **Specific muscles:** Fibularis (peroneus) longus, brevis
- **Joint actions:** Subtalar eversion (sole turns out)

**B. Equipment**

| Machine | Exact machine | Dumbbell | Barbell | Cable | Band | Bodyweight alternative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| No | — | No | No | Alternative | Yes (anchored to the inside) | Single-leg balance |

**C. Starting position**

- **Body posture:** Seated, leg straight.
- **Foot position:** Band around forefoot, anchored to the INSIDE (or loop around both feet).
- **Hands / grip:** Support.
- **Starting range:** Foot turned in.

**D. Execution and form**

1. **Start.** Set band.
2. **Initiate.** Turn the sole outward.
3. **Movement path.** Movement from the ankle only.
4. **Breathing.** Breathe.
5. **Contraction.** Hold 1 s.
6. **End position.** Sole facing out.
7. **Return.** Return slowly.
8. **Recommended ROM.** Full eversion range.

**E. Technique details**

- **Posture and alignment:** Knee still.
- **Tempo:** 1 s turn, 1 s hold, 3 s return.
- **Muscle engagement:** The fibularis muscles resist the inversion ankle sprain; strength and balance training are standard parts of sprain rehabilitation.

| Common mistake | How to correct it |
|---|---|
| Rotating the hip | Hold shin still |
| Band slipping toward the toes | Loop band around the mid-foot, not the toes |
| Moving too fast | Slow 3-second return |

**F. Training parameters**

| Beginner | Intermediate | Advanced | Rest | Progression |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 × 15 | 3 × 15–20 | Heavier band | 30 s | Heavier band |

**G. Evaluation**

| Hypertrophy | Strength | Beginner suitability | Stability demand | Equipment access | Safety |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Low | Low | Excellent | Low | Excellent | Very safe |

- **Advantages:** Protects against inversion sprains
- **Disadvantages:** Low load

**H. Alternatives**
